##### A Humble Request

Dear Reader,
Thank you so much for joining me on this road trip through Stoicism and exploring how its
timeless wisdom can help us overcome anxiety, fear, and depression. I genuinely hope that the
insights and techniques shared in this book have provided you with valuable tools for navigating
life's challenges and finding a sense of inner calm.
If you found this book helpful, I would be incredibly grateful if you would take a moment to share
your thoughts by leaving a review on Amazon. Your feedback not only helps me improve but also
helps others discover the benefits of Stoicism. Plus, it’s a wonderful way to pay it forward and
contribute to a community of like-minded individuals seeking peace and resilience.
Think of your review as a small act of kindness that can make a big difference. Whether it’s a few
sentences or a detailed account of your experience, every review counts. And who knows? Your
words might be just what someone else needs to embark on their own journey to tranquility.
Thank you again for your support and for being a part of this adventure. Wishing you continued
strength, wisdom, and serenity.
Warmest regards,
Ross
~ With Deep and Sincere Gratitude ~
"To live happily is an inward power of the soul.” Marcus Aurelius, Meditations
"He who is not happy with little will never be happy with much.” Epictetus, Discourses
"He is a man of sense who does not grieve for what he has not, but rejoices in what he has.” Epictetus, Discourses
"Do not try to seem wise to others. If you want to live a wise life, live it on your own terms and be grateful for it.” Epictetus, Enchiridion
"Nothing is more honorable than a grateful heart.” Seneca, Letters to Lucilius
"The greatest blessings of mankind are within us and within our reach. A wise man is content with his lot, whatever it may be, without wishing for what he has not.” Seneca, Letters to Lucilius
"All the good are friends of one another.” Zeno of Citium

##### Diving Deeper into Stoicism:

##### Your Gateway to More Wisdom and Insight

Discover more about the profound depths of Stoic philosophy with these carefully curated
resources. Whether you’re seeking more detailed texts, practical advice, or community discussions,
these resources will enrich your understanding and enhance your exploration into the wonderful
world of Stoicism.
BOOKS
Classic Stoic Texts
Meditations by Marcus Aurelius
Reference: Aurelius, M. (2006). Meditations. Translated by G. Hays. Modern Library. ISBN:
978-0812968255
Description: This personal journal of Roman Emperor Marcus Aurelius provides insights into his
Stoic practices and philosophy.
Letters from a Stoic by Seneca Reference: Seneca, L. A. (2004). Letters from a Stoic. Translated by R. Campbell. Penguin
Classics. ISBN: 978-0140442106
Description: A collection of letters offering practical advice on how to approach daily challenges
with Stoic wisdom.
Discourses and Selected Writings by Epictetus
Reference: Epictetus. (2008). The Discourses of Epictetus. Translated by R. Hard. Everyman’s
Library. ISBN: 978-0375725608
Description: This work captures the teachings of Epictetus, a former slave turned Stoic
philosopher, on living a life of virtue and wisdom.
The Enchiridion by Epictetus Reference: Epictetus. (1995). The Art of Living: The Classical Manual on Virtue, Happiness, and
Effectiveness. Translated by S. Lebell. HarperOne. ISBN: 978-0062513465
Description: A concise manual of Stoic ethical advice, offering practical guidance on how to live
a fulfilling life.
Modern Interpretations
A Guide to the Good Life: The Ancient Art of Stoic Joy by William B. Irvine Reference: Irvine, W. B. (2009). A Guide to the Good Life: The Ancient Art of Stoic Joy. Oxford
University Press. ISBN: 978-0195374612
Description: A modern take on Stoicism, providing practical advice on how to apply Stoic
principles to achieve a fulfilling life.
How to Be a Stoic: Using Ancient Philosophy to Live a Modern Life by Massimo Pigliucci Reference: Pigliucci, M. (2017). How to Be a Stoic: Using Ancient Philosophy to Live a Modern
Life. Basic Books. ISBN: 978-0465097951
Description: Pigliucci explores how to apply Stoic teachings to everyday challenges in the
contemporary world.
The Daily Stoic: 366 Meditations on Wisdom, Perseverance, and the Art of Living by Ryan
Holiday and Stephen Hanselman Reference: Holiday, R., & Hanselman, S. (2016). The Daily Stoic: 366 Meditations on Wisdom,
Perseverance, and the Art of Living. Portfolio. ISBN: 978-0735211735
Description: A daily devotional providing quotes from Stoic philosophers alongside reflections
and practical advice.
How to Think Like a Roman Emperor: The Stoic Philosophy of Marcus Aurelius by
Donald Robertson Reference: Robertson, D. (2018). How to Think Like a Roman Emperor: The Stoic Philosophy of
Marcus Aurelius. St. Martin's Press. ISBN: 978-1250196620
Description: A blend of biography and self-help that applies the teachings of Marcus Aurelius to
modern life.
Comprehensive Anthologies
The Stoic Philosophy of Seneca: Essays and Letters Reference: Seneca, L. A. (1997). The Stoic Philosophy of Seneca: Essays and Letters. Translated
by R. M. Gummere. University of Chicago Press. ISBN: 978-0226748429
Description: A collection of Seneca’s essays and letters that cover a wide range of Stoic themes.
The Hellenistic Philosophers, Vol. 1: Translations of the Principal Sources with
Philosophical Commentary by A. A. Long and D. N. Sedley Reference: Long, A. A., & Sedley, D. N. (1987). The Hellenistic Philosophers, Vol. 1:
Translations of the Principal Sources with Philosophical Commentary. Cambridge University
Press. ISBN: 978-0521275569
Description: A comprehensive anthology that includes translations and commentary on the
works of the major Hellenistic philosophers, including the Stoics.
